体增速。
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季
度扩大 0.9 个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固
定资产投资同比降幅约为 16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产
后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百
分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，指向投资弱化不只
是地产问题。
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计
投资下降 2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公
布的 13 个制造业行业中，仅纺织业、铁路船舶航空航天和计算机通信电
子设备制造业投资保持正增长；正增长行业数量由前三季度的 5 个减少至
3 个。具体看，一方面，优势产业仍然具备需求、生产和资本开支相互支
撑的条件。更宽口径的高技术产业投资增长 4.0%，其中信息服务、航空
航天设备、电子及通信设备投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%和 6.6%。另一

方面，部分上游原料料行业和下游制造业投资维持负增。农副食品、食
品、化工、医药、金属制品、专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降
4.1%、10.7%、8.4%、9.2%、9.9%、9.0%和 7.9%。
地产投资降幅扩大，现房销售制度下销售回款向开发投资传导节奏出现变
化。10 月，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%，降幅较前三季度扩大 1.0 个
百分点；按累计金额反推，10 月单月投资下降约 33.2%，较 9 月约
26.3%的降幅进一步扩大。结构上看，一方面， 地产销售存在局部企
稳。销售端看，10 月，单月商品房销售面积和销售额分别下降约 19.1%
和 18.6%，降幅较 9 月分别收窄约 1.6 个和 1.5 个百分点；二手房交易网
签面积累计增长 11.4%。价格端看，10 月，一、二、三线城市新房价格
同比降幅分别收窄 0.4 个、0.4 个和 0.3 个百分点，二手房价格同比降幅
也均有收窄；环比看，一线城市新房价格环比由上涨转为持平，二线城市
由持平转为下降，或指向价格整体修复动能不足。另一方面，现房销售制

度改革背景下销售回款对投资的前置融资功能减弱。资金端看，1—10 月
房地产开发企业到位资金下降 21.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.3 个百分
点。其中，国内贷款和自筹资金分别下降 33.4%和 21.9%，定金及预收款
下降 15.5%，个人按揭贷款下降 22.1%。到位资金降幅明显大于商品房销
售额 14.3%的累计降幅，一定程度上表明销售收入未同步转化为开发企业
可以用于投资的有效资金。供给端看，1—10 月房屋施工面积下降
13.0%，竣工面积下降 26.6%，降幅较前三季度分别扩大 0.2 个和 1.5 个
百分点；新开工面积下降 24.4%，降幅小幅收窄，仍处较低水平。
基建延续回落，资金尚未转化为实物工作量。10 月，基建投资累计同比
下降 5.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9 个百分点。其中，电热燃水投资下
降 7.7%，交通运输仓储邮政投资增长 0.7%，水利环境和公共设施管理投
资下降 14.4%，均较前三季度有所放缓。我们分析，政策资金到项目建设
存在传导时滞是一个重要原因。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
内部尚存在结构性支撑。1—10 月信息传输业、航空运输业和水上运输业
投资分别增长 32.0%、12.5%和 12.5%，数字基础设施及部分交通网络仍
保持较快增长。 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长
0.4%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮
消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4
个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范围扩大。10 月汽车类零
售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零售额增长
2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由
31.1%降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降
28.7%，建筑装潢材料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧
性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务
零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通讯信息服务、旅游咨
询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧性。 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重
点正由资金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提
出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进度，用好用足各项政策性资金，
及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业扩大设备更新和无
形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的堵
点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提
出加强消费线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消
费基础设施建设政策对文旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部
署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

风险提示：实物工作量形成不及预期、AI 产业链超预期降温、外需边际走
弱。

1. 生产：工业生产增速回落，外需支撑优势制造业高增 
工业生产回落。10 月，规上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，
环比增长 0.2%，低于季节性。分门类看，采矿业、制造业和电热燃水业增加值分别增长
0.7%、5.4%和 4.4%，较 9 月分别下降 0.6 个、0.6 个和 1.2 个百分点，工业生产呈现较
为广泛的降温。我们分析工业生产放缓主要有两方面拖累，一是地产、基建及部分传统
制造业需求继续走弱，生产改善不具备向更广泛行业扩散的条件。10 月，非金制品、黑
金冶炼和有色冶炼增加值分别下降 2.3%、1.7%和 4.4%；钢材、水泥、平板玻璃和十种
有色金属产量分别下降 5.1%、1.3%、16.0%和 1.2%。化工和橡胶塑料制品增加值也仅
增长 1.3%和 1.2%。汽车制造业增加值增速由 6.7%降至 0.9%，汽车产量下降 7.7%。
二是国际能源价格再次上涨强化中下游制造业成本约束，可能对部分行业扩产形成压制。
10 月布伦特原油现货均价升至每桶 124 美元，较 9 月上涨约 10 美元。同期工业生产者
购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，高于出厂价格 5.7%的涨幅，其中燃料动力类和化工原料类购
进价格分别上涨 18.6%和 16.8%；制造业 PMI 原材料购进价格指数为 56.8%，仍处于
较高扩张区间。从生产数据看，10 月原油加工量下降 8.1%，化工、橡胶塑料等成本敏
感行业增加值仅低位增长。我们分析，油价上涨未必是工业生产回落的直接原因，但叠
加内需偏弱、成本传导不畅，可能会放大成本约束，挤压部分中下游企业的扩产空间。  
工业生产主要增量依旧集中于外需强劲背景下的优势制造领域。10 月，规模以上工
业企业出口交货值增长 18.6%，较 9 月提高 1.6 个百分点；装备制造业出口交货值增长
20.6%，其中汽车、电气机械和电子行业分别增长 52.2%、23.7%和 15.3%，三者对全
部工业出口交货值增长的贡献率合计达到 66.7%。与出口结构相对应，高技术制造业和
装备制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%，计算机通信电子设备、专用设备、铁路船
舶航空航天设备制造业分别增长 19.4%、11.5%和 9.2%；集成电路、工业机器人和民用
钢质船舶产量分别增长 21.9%、26.0%和 35.6%。 
服务业小幅放缓，生产性和数字化服务仍是主要支撑。10 月，服务业生产指数同比
增长 4.6%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.0%，较 9 月下降 0.2
个百分点。结构上看，生产性和数字化服务增速较高。租赁和商务服务业、信息传输软
件和信息技术服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.3%、9.2%和 5.1%，
均高于服务业整体增速。

2. 投资：延续回落 
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9
个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固定资产投资同比降幅约为
16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降
幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，
指向投资弱化不只是地产问题。

制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计投资下降
2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公布的 13 个制造业行业中，

3. 消费：再度转弱 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下
降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%
和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范
围扩大。10 月汽车类零售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零
售额增长 2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由 31.1%
降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降 28.7%，建筑装潢材
料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，
高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通
讯信息服务、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧
性。

4. 展望：实物工作量形成是重点 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重点正由资
金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性金融
工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进
度，用好用足各项政策性资金，及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业
扩大设备更新和无形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的
堵点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提出加强消费
线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消费基础设施建设政策对文
旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价（或行业
指数）相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。其中 A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准；新三板市场以三板成指（针
对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

Document 022: 2000年历史重演，反弹之后才是真正的崩跌？

Source PDF: all report pdf/2000年历史重演，反弹之后才是真正的崩跌？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

TS Lombard 警告，当前科技股抛售向⼤盘蔓延的⾛势，与 2000 年互联⽹泡沫破裂前⼣惊
⼈相似：科技股先跌、市场反弹，随后危机进⼀步扩⼤。尽管当前科技巨头盈利和基本⾯
更强，估值泡沫也已部分消化，但 AI 投资热潮的乐观叙事与当年如出⼀辙，投资者不应低
估反弹后再度⼤跌的⻛险。
摘要：
此轮科技股抛售正在向更⼴泛市场蔓延，令部分分析师想起了互联⽹泡沫破裂前⼣的市场形
态。
伦敦经济研究机构 TS Lombard 的分析显⽰，当前市场⾛势与 2000 年互联⽹泡沫崩溃前的路径存
在令⼈不安的相似之处 —— 科技股率先下跌，⼤盘随后跟进，⽽历史上真正的崩跌恰恰发⽣在
⼀轮显著反弹之后。
该机构宏观策略师 Dario Perkins 警告称， "与 2000 年夏天、崩盘前⼣存在⼀些令⼈忧虑的相似之
处。 "
⽬前，标普 500 指数正⾯临 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最差单周表现，此前数周抛售主要集中于科技板块，
如今已明显向更⼴泛市场扩散。与此同时，科技板块在标普 500 指数中的权重已超过 40% ，较

2000 年峰值的 35% 更⾼，若将 Meta Platforms 和 Alphabet 等被归⼊通信服务板块的科技巨头⼀
并计⼊，这⼀⽐例上⽉更突破 46% ，意味着科技板块对整体市场的潜在冲击⼒远超当年。
历史镜鉴：反弹掩盖了真正的转折
据彭博报道， TS Lombard 的分析还原了 2000 年的市场演变路径：纳斯达克综合指数于当年 3 ⽉
10 ⽇触顶，随后⾄ 4 ⽉中旬跌逾三分之⼀，但此后⾄ 6 ⽉中旬⼜反弹 35%—— 投资者趁低买⼊，
并将资⾦转向盈利能⼒较强的⼤型科技公司。
彼时，距纳斯达克⻅顶已过去六个⽉，标普 500 指数仍处于历史⾼位。
然⽽，真正的破坏随后才开始显现。
较弱科技企业的融资环境收紧、信⽤利差扩⼤、盈利预期下调，开始拖累那些以科技公司为客
⼾的盈利型企业。纳斯达克在此后三个⽉内再度暴跌逾 40% 。Perkins 指出， " 瓶颈并不是看多的
理由 —— 它们可能预⽰着⼀场⼤规模的供给过剩。 " 他补充说，当时看似健康的订单簿掩盖了即
将到来的转折。

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
当前⾛势：抛售从科技蔓延⾄⼤盘
此轮下跌的导⽕索，是市场对⼈⼯智能过度投资的担忧，以及⺠主党横扫国会后的政策不确定
性。纳斯达克⾃ 10 ⽉初的历史⾼点回落后持续承压，标普 500 起初表现相对抗跌，但如今已难以
独善其⾝。
科技板块在指数中的超⾼权重，放⼤了其对整体市场的传导⻛险。信息技术板块权重上⽉已超
过标普 500 的 40% ，超越 2000 年 35% 的历史峰值；若将 Meta Platforms 和 Alphabet 纳⼊统计，⼴
义科技板块的权重上⽉累计超过 46% 。
这意味着，⼀旦科技板块持续承压，对⼤盘的拖累效应将远⽐ 26 年前更为显著。
今昔差异：估值泡沫已提前消化
当然， 2026 年与 2000 年之间也存在重要差异。
2000 年互联⽹泡沫⾼峰期，⼤量上市公司根本没有盈利，即便是盈利企业，市场也给出了极为

离谱的估值溢价 —— 信息技术板块的 12 个⽉远期市盈率⼀度接近 60 倍。
相⽐之下，当前主要科技公司普遍录得强劲盈利，估值溢价远不及当年极端。
更重要的是，互联⽹泡沫时期积累的估值泡沫花了数年才消散，⽽此轮科技股的估值虚⾼看起
来已经提前出清。
此外，⽀撑⼈⼯智能⾏情的基本⾯依然存在：超⼤规模云计算⼚商尚未下调强劲的资本⽀出计
划，算⼒需求依然旺盛，盈利增⻓保持强劲，宏观经济整体健康。
分析师提⽰：相似的叙事本⾝就是⻛险
尽管如此， TS Lombard 的 Perkins 仍提醒投资者保持警惕。
他指出，该机构⾃⾝⽀持⼈⼯智能扩张可持续性的论据，与 2000 年互联⽹泡沫破裂时市场上流
⾏的乐观叙事 " 惊⼈地相似 " 。
" 这⾄少应该让我们保持⼀份谦逊，"Perkins 说。
在他看来，历史上这⼀模式的演绎⽅式值得铭记：市场先经历⼀轮调整，随后迅速反弹，⽽真
正的崩跌往往在此之后才姗姗来迟。对于当前正在经历剧烈波动和深度亏损的投资者⽽⾔，这
⼀警⽰或许⽐任何技术指标都更值得认真对待。

Document 024: 2026 has exposed new risks for the European gas market (2026-11-09)

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026 has exposed new risks for the European gas market (2026-11-09).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026EDITORIAL
2026 HAS EXPOSED NEW RISKS FOR THE EUROPEAN GAS MARKET
1 C. Grekou, V. Mignon, L. Ragot, 2026, ‘From Russian gas to the global LNG market: The European Union’s new vulnerability’, CEPII, Policy Brief.
2 ACER, 2026, ‘Analysis of the European LNG market development. Monitoring report’.
3 These contracts allow the buyer to choose, or change, the delivery point for the shipment. Recent US contracts (Free On Board and flexible destination) generally

feature these characteristics.
4 IEA, 2026, ‘Global Gas Security Review & Gas Market Report Q4-2026’.
Europe’s vulnerability to the gas market has changed in recent years. Whilst its reliance on imports has remained stable,
its gas supply has been more vulnerable to global market conditions since 2022, due to a shift in the composition of its
imports. Furthermore, in 2026, the combination of high geopolitical risk in one of the main production regions and the

pressure of climate change on European consumers has created an unprecedented situation, leading to price pressures
and significant uncertainty regarding the short-term outlook. Even if they ease, tensions in the European gas market could
persist beyond the winter, largely due to the need to refill inventories.
Whilst Europe is not heavily reliant on gas imports from the Middle
East, it has had to contend with a fresh surge in prices since the out -

break of war in Iran last February, a rise that has, in part, been passed
on to electricity prices this summer. Furthermore, new sources of
vulnerability, particularly those linked to climate change, are at play.
These call for caution regarding the short-term outlook for the Euro -
pean gas market.
EUROPE’S GROWING EXPOSURE TO MARKET VOLATILITY
Unlike the 2022 energy crisis, which hit Europe directly, the war in
Iran is indirectly affecting the European gas market. 80% of gas exports

from the Middle East are destined for Asia. However, the growing inter-
connection between the two main gas-importing regions — Asia and
Europe — has resulted in the crisis spreading to Europe. Since 2022,
the transmission of shocks to liquefied natural gas (LNG) benchmark
prices on the European and Asian markets (TTF and JKM) has increased
significantly, in both directions1.
Despite a fall of around 20% in European gas consumption since 2022,
the replacement of around 60% of Russian pipeline imports with pur -

chases of liquefied natural gas (see Chart 1) has kept Europe’s vulne -
rability at a high level. Given developments in the LNG market —
growing interconnection between European and Asian markets and
the importance of spot contracts (around one-third of European supply
contracts2) — Europe finds itself, through no fault of its own, increa -
singly exposed to gas price volatility.
Furthermore, the increasing autonomy and flexibility of the global
LNG market may exacerbate this volatility. The higher proportion of

LNG sales contracts with flexible destinations3 (rising from 29% to 51%
of the total between 2016 and 2025), linked to the massive influx of
US LNG onto the market, together with the reduction in the share of
contracts indexed to oil prices (from 70% to 55% over the same period),
have made price formation on the LNG market more autonomous, the-
reby increasing the opportunities for arbitrage and the flexibility of this
market.
MAJOR DISRUPTIONS IN THE LNG MARKET HAVE CONSTRAINED THE REFIL-

LING OF EUROPEAN INVENTORIES
The 85% reduction in LNG exports from Qatar and the UAE in Q2 and Q3
2026 was offset by two-thirds through, on the one hand, an increase
in exports from other producing countries (primarily the United States)
and, on the other hand, a fall in demand in Asia and Europe (which
is forecast to reach, respectively, 1.5% and 3% year-on-year in 2026,
according to IEA forecasts4). Owing to the damage caused by the war to
infrastructure (17% of Qatar’s production capacity is out of service) and

logistical constraints specific to the LNG market (unavailability of LNG
carriers), the LNG market has benefited only marginally from the re -
sumption of flows through the Strait of Hormuz since July. Against this
backdrop of tensions in the gas market, high prices in Europe (up by
around 174% since the outbreak of the conflict) have significantly
slowed the refilling of European stocks. They stood at 75% of full capa-
city at the end of October, whilst the official target is 80% (see Chart 2).

OVER THE WINTER, DIVERGENT FACTORS COULD STABILISE PRICES AT A
HIGH LEVEL
Gas stock levels that are below the target set by the European Commis-
sion do not increase the risk of a shortage on the European continent.
Nevertheless, Europe’s vulnerability towards the end of winter, in the
event of a cold snap, would force it to buy on the market at high prices.

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Furthermore, from a technical perspective, exposure to the conse -
quences of a cold snap at the end of winter is all the more pronounced
as the rate at which gas can be withdrawn from storage (the ease with
which a country can access its supplies) decreases (due to lower stock
levels in storage facilities).
Apart from stock levels at the start of winter, three factors will deter -

mine the situation at the end of the season, a key period of vulnera -
bility:
1/ the resumption of exports from the Gulf, a region that is essential for
restoring market balance in the short term;
2/ weather conditions during the winter;
3/ renewable energy production.
According to the IEA, in the worst-case scenario (with exports from the
Middle East only resuming from the end of the first quarter of 2027, a
cold winter and low renewable energy production), stocks would stand

at 7% of total capacity by the end of winter, compared with 33% in
the best-case scenario. Such levels remain significantly below the ave-
rage for the last five years at this time of year: 80% in the worst-case
scenario and 18% in the best-case scenario. This would underpin res -
tocking demand over the next two quarters, and therefore keep prices
at a fairly high level. For the time being, a significant recovery in LNG
exports from the Gulf remains highly uncertain, even though indirect

talks between the warring parties have resumed. On a more positive
note, the current development of a very powerful El Niño event should,
in principle, be associated with relatively mild temperatures during the
winter in western Europe. According to the futures markets, European
gas prices are not expected to return to their pre-crisis levels until
2028 (see Chart 3).
ARE WE HEADING FOR A SUMMER PEAK IN GAS DEMAND?
In addition to these factors, which are likely to prolong market tensions

during the first half of 2027, the summer period could also be cha -
racterised by increased demand for gas. During the summer of 2026,
5 In early October 2026, the water levels in French reservoirs reached a ten-year low, standing at 23% below seasonal averages.
6 RTE data.
7 M. Mueller, P. Devaux, October 2026, ‘What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity
continues to turn the screw?’, EcoInsight.

8 Oxford Institute for Energy Studies, October 2026, Quarterly Gas Market Review.
weather conditions hampered the generation of certain forms of re -
newable energy (heatwaves and droughts restricting electricity gene -
ration from hydroelectric5 and wind power sources). They also disrup-
ted nuclear power generation (in France, nuclear electricity generation
fell by around 10% year-on-year between August and October6). Along-
side these constraints on carbon-free electricity generation, heatwaves

are driving up demand for electricity, particularly for air conditioning.
Against this backdrop, as a source of flexibility within the electricity
mix, gas remains a key determinant of electricity prices in Europe 7.
In Q3 2026, whilst the decline in European gas consumption mainly
affected industry, the residential sector and the commercial sector 8,
consumption by gas-fired power stations was 9% higher than in 2025.
STRENGTHENING EUROPEAN PROGRAMMES

In this context, and despite the progress made in decarbonising the
European electricity mix, high gas prices have led to a rise in whole -
sale electricity prices in most European countries. Between July and
September 2026, these prices were on average 54% higher than in the
same period a year earlier.
Against this backdrop of widespread rises in energy prices, European go-
vernments have primarily introduced measures to support households’
purchasing power when buying fuel. Measures relating to gas supply

remain fairly limited. The European Commission has extended the tar-
get for refilling European stocks to 80% (instead of 90%) for 2026. It has
also urged Member States to extend the stock-refill season in order
to smooth out purchase prices. Furthermore, the measures taken in
2022 under the RePower EU programme have been maintained: redu -
cing dependence on Russian gas with the aim of a complete phase-out
(pipeline and LNG) by the end of 2027; and pooling gas purchases at

European level. The ‘decarbonisation and electrification’ component of
this programme was strengthened in April 2026 by the Accelerate EU
programme, notably through measures to accelerate the electrification
of demand and the development of electricity grid infrastructure at
European level.

At national level, it is worth highlighting the price cap mechanism for
gas purchased by power stations, which the Italian government is set
to introduce. In 2025, gas set the price of electricity in Italy in 73% of
cases (compared with an average of 40% across Europe). This price cap
mechanism, similar to that introduced in Spain in 2022, is, in principle,

only sustainable in the short term. Indeed, its cost is likely to be signi-
ficant given the Italian electricity mix’s high dependence on gas.
*****
Increased market dependence and the consequences of rising climate
risk for European electricity generation are altering the vulnerabilities
associated with the continent’s gas market. The sources of risk are
increasing in number and becoming harder to manage. This calls for
the continuation and strengthening of decarbonisation efforts in the

European policies launched in 2022.
Pascal Devaux
pascal.devaux@bnpparibas.com

### Page 1

1
研究员：万亮 邮箱：xnqh_wl@swfutures.com
期货从业证书号：F03116714 交易咨询从业证书号：Z0019298
时间：2026.11.17
出口高位运行、PPI 涨幅扩大，内需依然偏弱
——2026 年10 月宏观数据分析

### Page 2

2
我们梳理了 2026 年 10月国内重要的宏观经济数据，以增加对当前宏观经
济运行节奏的理解。
总的来说，2026年 10月国内经济延续了外需强、内需弱，生产转弱、需
求不足的格局。10月制造业采购经理指数回落至 49.3%，重回收缩区间；工业
增加值、社会消费品零售总额增速同步放缓，固定资产投资和房地产销售降幅
进一步扩大；与此同时，进出口维持高增，PPI同比涨幅扩大至 5.7%，M1 增
速明显回升，外需与物价的表现明显好于内需。
对此，我们认为，需要理性客观的看待当前宏观经济 K型分化形势。新经
济保持高景气度，但尚不足以对冲房地产和基建等传统经济的下行，国内经济
的复苏不能一蹴而就。当前外部不稳定不确定因素较多，国内有效需求不足，
经济运行面临不少挑战。这要求实施更加积极有为的宏观政策，持续扩大内需、
优化供给，推动经济实现质的有效提升和量的合理增长。当前房地产市场处在
企稳复苏的关键节点，后续对宏观经济的拖累有望显著收窄。整体而言，2026
年面临的宏观环境好于 2025 年，但强劲复苏的可能性不大。

一、制造业 PMI 重回收缩区间
10 月份，制造业采购经理指数（PMI）为 49.3%，比上月下降 0.8 个百分
点，重回收缩区间。从企业规模看，大型企业 PMI 为 50.2%，比上月下降 0.4
个百分点，仍高于临界点；中、小型企业 PMI分别为 48.4%和 47.5%，比上月
下降 1.3个和 1.4个百分点，均低于临界点。
从分类指数看，构成制造业 PMI的 5个分类指数中，仅供应商配送时间指
数高于临界点，生产指数、新订单指数、原材料库存指数和从业人员指数均低
于临界点。生产指数为 49.8%，比上月下降 1.9 个百分点，表明制造业生产活

### Page 3

3
动有所放缓。新订单指数为 49.4%，比上月下降 1.1 个百分点，表明制造业市
场需求景气度下降。原材料库存指数为 48.4%，比上月上升 0.2 个百分点，表
明制造业主要原材料库存量降幅有所收窄。从业人员指数为 48.4%，与上月持
平，表明制造业企业用工景气度总体平稳。供应商配送时间指数为 50.2%，比
上月上升 0.1个百分点，表明制造业原材料供应商交货时间加快。
10月份，非制造业商务活动指数为 49.7%，比上月下降 0.5个百分点，非
制造业景气水平有所下降。分行业看，建筑业商务活动指数为 48.0%，比上月
下降 2.3个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.0%，比上月下降 0.2个百分点。
建筑业新订单指数降至 40.0%，比上月下降 5.7 个百分点，是本月非制造业景
气回落的主要拖累。
总的来说，10月制造业 PMI环比下降 0.8个百分点，重回收缩区间。其中
生产指数大幅回落 1.9 个百分点至 49.8%，新订单指数降至 49.4%，需求不足
对生产的制约进一步显现。大型企业 PMI仍在扩张区间，中、小型企业则加速

收缩，企业规模之间的景气分化有所扩大。非制造业商务活动指数同步回落至
49.7%，建筑业新订单指数降至 40.0%，稳投资政策的效果尚未在景气指标中体
图 1：中国制造业 PMI
（数据来源：ifind 资讯）

现。
二、10月 CPI 同比上涨 0.7%，PPI 同比上涨 5.7%
2026年 10月份，全国居民消费价格同比上涨 0.7%。其中，城市上涨 0.7%，
农村上涨 0.7%；食品价格下降 2.9%，非食品价格上涨 1.5%；消费品价格上涨
0.8%，服务价格上涨 0.7%。扣除食品和能源价格的核心 CPI 同比上涨 0.8%，
涨幅比上月回落 0.2个百分点。
10月份，全国居民消费价格环比持平。其中，城市持平，农村下降 0.1%；
食品价格下降 1.0%，非食品价格上涨 0.2%；消费品价格下降 0.1%，服务价格
上涨 0.1%。
10月份，食品烟酒及在外餐饮类价格同比下降 1.6%，影响 CPI下降约 0.48
个百分点。食品中，鲜菜价格下降 16.9%，影响 CPI下降约 0.31 个百分点；畜
肉类价格下降 2.7%，影响 CPI下降约 0.11个百分点，其中猪肉价格下降 8.6%；
鲜果价格下降 3.8%，影响 CPI 下降约 0.07 个百分点；蛋类价格上涨 10.1%，

影响 CPI上涨约 0.05 个百分点。其他七大类价格同比六涨一降。其中，交通通
信、医疗保健、其他用品及服务价格分别上涨 5.5%、2.4%和 1.8%，教育文化
娱乐、衣着、生活用品及服务价格分别上涨 1.5%、1.1%和 0.2%；居住价格下
降 0.3%。

三、出口高位运行，进口明显提速
以美元计，10月，中国进出口总值 7193.8亿美元，同比增长 37.9%；出口
总值 4123.3亿美元，同比增长 35.2%；进口总值 3070.5亿美元，同比增长 41.6%；
贸易顺差 1052.8亿美元。出口增速较上月小幅回落 1.1个百分点，仍处历史高
位；进口增速较上月加快 10.6个百分点，明显提速。
其中，10月，集成电路出口额同比增长 137.1%（前值为 136%）、自动数

据处理设备及其零部件出口额同比增长 114.8%（前值为 92.9%）。国内进出口
均受益于全球 AI 投资的高景气度。以人民币计，10月货物进出口总额 48683
亿元，同比增长 31.2%。

政策在 10月集中落地，成为支撑社融的主要力量。1-10月社融存量增速 7.2%，
高于人民币贷款增速 4.7%，两者剪刀差扩大至 2.5个百分点。

环比回升 1.2个百分点。M1-M2剪刀差由-4.0%收窄至-2.7%，资金活化程度有
所改善。
总的来说，2026年 10月金融数据呈现总量偏弱、结构分化的特征。人民
币贷款单月净减少，居民贷款全面收缩、企业票据融资大幅压降，反映实体部
门的融资需求依然不振；社融存量增速回升主要依靠政府债券放量，财政扩张
仍是信用扩张的主要来源。M1 增速明显回升、剪刀差收窄，与财政资金集中
拨付和企业活期存款改善有关，但能否传导至实体投资需求仍待观察。

394637万平方米，下降 13.3%。房屋新开工面积 37036万平方米，下降
24.4%，降幅比 1-9月份收窄 0.5个百分点。其中，住宅新开工面积 26959万
平方米，下降 24.9%。房屋竣工面积 25544万平方米，下降 26.6%，降幅比 1-
9 月份扩大 1.5 个百分点。其中，住宅竣工面积 17914万平方米，下降
27.9%。
10月末，商品房待售面积 74412万平方米，同比下降 1.7%。其中，待售
3 年以下面积 53652万平方米，下降 5.3%。狭义的商品房待售面积库存继续
下降，库存持续去化。

（数据来源：ifind 资讯）
图 12：中国商品房待售面积

### Page 14

14
2026年 10月房地产销售面积、投资降幅继续扩大，新开工降幅略有收窄。
我们仍然认为，房地产市场的企稳复苏是一个漫长的过程，难以一蹴而就。当
前，房地产市场已处于底部阶段，房地产新开工面积、投资等或已处于长期均
衡水平下。随着基数的下降，后续商品房销售面积、销售额同比增速降幅将逐
步收窄。随着市场预期的转变，本轮房地产下行周期的“市场底”正在出现。
尽管存在巨大的分歧，2026年有望成为房地产市场止跌回稳的关键节点。
积极因素在逐步累积，房地产对宏观经济和资本市场的拖累将显著收窄。
七、总结与展望
总的来说，2026年 10月国内经济延续了外需强、内需弱，生产转弱、需
求不足的格局。前三季度国内生产总值同比增长 4.6%。10月制造业 PMI回落
至 49.3%，重回收缩区间，工业增加值增速放缓至 4.8%，社会消费品零售总额
增速回落至 0.4%，固定资产投资和房地产销售降幅进一步扩大；与此同时，出
口维持 35.2%的高增，进口增速加快至 41.6%，PPI同比涨幅扩大至 5.7%，M1

增速回升至 4.4%，外需与物价的表现明显好于内需。
对此，我们认为，当前外部不稳定不确定因素较多，国内有效需求不足，
经济运行面临不少挑战。这要求实施更加积极有为的宏观政策，持续扩大内需、
优化供给，推动经济实现质的有效提升和量的合理增长。当前房地产市场处在
企稳复苏的关键节点，后续对宏观经济的拖累有望显著收窄。整体而言，2026
年面临的宏观环境好于 2025 年，但国内经济强劲复苏的可能性不大。
跟踪后续政策的落地细节，观察物价端的回升力度，耐心等待宏观经济的
向上信号。

予測

＜実質成長率：2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想＞

１. 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、民間消費の落ち込みを主因として前期比年率▲0.1％と 4
四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。 
２. 物価上昇ペースの加速に伴う実質購買力の低下を主因として民間消費が低迷することな
どから、2026年 10-12月期、2027年 1-3月期の実質 GDP成長率は 0％台前半から半ばに
とどまることが予想される。2026年度後半の日本経済は低空飛行が続く可能性が高い。 
３. 2027年度に入ると、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下から家計の実
質購買力が改善し、民間消費が回復に向かうほか、原材料価格の低下に伴う企業収益の
改善を受けて設備投資の増勢ペースが加速することが見込まれる。 
４. 実質 GDP成長率は 2026年度が 0.9％、2027年度が 1.3％、2028年度が 1.1％と予想する。
消費税率引き下げによる実質 GDPへの影響は 2026年度が▲0.1％、2027年度が 0.3％と
試算する。 
５. 消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品を除く総合）は輸入物価の上昇が食料、日用品など幅広い
品目に波及し、2026年度末にかけては 3％台まで加速するが、飲食料品の消費税率引き
下げを受けて、2027年度には 1％台まで鈍化するだろう。消費者物価上昇率（生鮮食品
を除く総合）は、2026年度が 2.4％、2027年度が 1.5％、2028年度が 2.0％と予想する。

### Page 2

（中東情勢の影響） 
中東情勢の緊迫化を受けて一時大幅に減少した日本の原油輸入量は、代替調達の進展によって持
ち直し、7月以降は前年の水準を上回って推移している。 
財務省の「貿易統計」で原油輸入の動向を確認すると、2026年 3月は攻撃前にホルムズ海峡を通
過・出港していた原油が日本に到着したため、影響はまだ限定的であったが、4、5月には中東産を
中心に原油輸入量が前年比で 6割前後の大幅減少となった。その後、ホルムズ海峡を経由しない中
東産に加え、米国を中心とした代替調達が進んだことから、6月には減少幅が大きく縮小し、7月
以降は、原油輸入量が前年の水準を上回っている。2025年時点で中東産が約 7割を占めていたナフ
サについては、中東からの輸入量は減少が続いているものの、米国、ペルー、アルジェリアからの
輸入量が急増するなど代替調達が進展している。

### Page 3

原油輸入に占める中東の割合（数量ベース）は 2025年時点で 94.0％だったが、足もとでは 60％
台まで低下し、米国の割合が 2025年の 3.8％から 30％台まで上昇している。また、ナフサ輸入に占
める中東の割合は 2026年 5月に 10％まで低下した後、7月以降は 50％前後まで持ち直している。
ただし、2025年の 73.8％と比べれば依然として低い水準にある。

価格面では、ドバイ原油の市況価格は 3月に 1バレル 130ドル近く（月中平均）まで急上昇した
後、6、7月には 80ドル前後まで低下した。その
後、8月に 80ドル台後半、9、10月には 110ドル
程度まで急上昇したが、11月には 100ドルを割り
込んでいる。一方、通関（入着）ベースの原油価
格は、市況価格の変動が遅れて反映されるが、3
月の 1バレル 60ドル台から 6月に 117ドルまで急
上昇した後、7月も 115ドルと高止まりした。8
月には 102ドル、9月には 101ドルまで低下した
ものの、市況価格が 6、7月に大きく低下したに
もかかわらず、通関価格は 9月まで 100ドルを上
回る高水準が続いている。 
原油の輸入量が前年の水準を回復する中、輸入価格が大幅に上昇したため、原油の輸入金額は 6
月に前年比で増加に転じた後、高い伸びが続いており、貿易赤字の拡大要因となっている。 
もともと、通関ベースの原油価格は、指標価格に調整金、タンカー運賃、保険料などが上乗せさ
れているが、中東情勢緊迫後は調整金、タンカー運賃が大幅に上昇したこと、代替調達先の米国か
らの輸入価格が中東を上回っていることなどから、市況価格との差が大きく拡大している。代替調
達が進展するもとでは、原油の輸入価格が市況価格ほど低下せずに貿易収支の悪化につながること
が見込まれる。

（AI関連財が輸出を牽引、交易条件の悪化を緩和） 
グローバルな AI関連需要の拡大を背景に、日本の輸出も AI関連財が牽引役となっている。財
務省の「貿易統計」をもとに、AI関連財1とそれ以外の財に分けて輸出動向を確認すると、輸出数
量全体はそれほど強くないものの、AI関連財は 2026年入り後、前年比二桁の高い伸びが続いてい
る。2026年 7-9月期の輸出数量は、AI関連財が前年比 13.7％と大きく増加した一方、非 AI関連財
は同 1.2％の小幅な増加にとどまった。AI関連財が輸出数量全体を下支えする構図となっている。 
AI関連財は数量だけでなく、価格の上昇も顕著となっている。世界的な AI投資の拡大に伴い、
半導体や電子部品などの需給の引き締まりがその背景にあると考えられる。2026年 7-9月期の AI
関連財の輸出価格は前年比 24.3％と大幅に上昇し、輸出数量の増加と合わせて輸出金額は同 38.0％

1 AI関連財は、WTOが公表する AI関連財（AI-Enabling-Products）103品目。2025年の輸出全体に占める AI関連財の割
合は 17.1％。

（年・四半期）
＜非AI関連財＞ ＜AI関連財＞

こうした AI関連財を中心とする輸出価格の上昇は、中東情勢の緊迫化に伴う交易条件の悪化を
緩和する役割も果たしている。原油をはじめとする資源価格の上昇は、輸入物価の上昇を通じて交
易条件を悪化させるが、今回は輸出物価も大幅に上昇している。このため、輸出物価を輸入物価で
除して求めた交易条件指数の悪化は比較的小幅にとどまっている。 
過去の資源価格高騰局面と比較すると、2005～2008年頃の世界的な資源価格高騰局面には、原
油などの輸入物価が大幅に上昇する一方、輸出物価の上昇がそれに追いつかず、交易条件が悪化し
た。また、2020年以降も、コロナ禍からの世界経済の回復に伴う資源価格の上昇に加え、2022年
のロシアによるウクライナ侵攻を受けた資源価格の急騰や円安の進行によって、交易条件が大幅に
悪化した。両局面ともに交易条件の累積的な悪化幅は▲30％以上となった。これに対し、2026年
1-3月期を起点とした 7-9月期までの 2四半期の悪化幅は▲6.6％である。AI関連財を中心とする輸
出価格の上昇が、原油価格の高騰に伴う輸入物価上昇の影響を一部相殺し、交易条件の悪化を緩和
している点が、過去の資源価格高騰局面との大き
な違いである。 
ただし、輸入物価の上昇率は輸出物価の上昇
率を上回っており、交易条件の悪化が続いている
ことに変わりはない。今後も原油価格が高止まり
する場合には、海外への所得流出を通じて国内経
済を下押しする可能性がある。一方、AI関連需
要の拡大を背景とした輸出価格の上昇が続けば、
交易条件の悪化を和らげる要因となるだろう。

（2027年の春闘賃上げ率は前年を若干上回る見込み） 
名目賃金を消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）で割り引いた実質賃金は、2026年 1月から
9ヵ月連続でプラスの伸びを維持している。名目賃金（現金給与総額）が 2025年中の 2％台前半か
ら 2026年入り後には 3％台へ伸びを高めたことに加え、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策の影響な
どから、物価上昇率が大きく縮小したことが実質賃金の押し上げに寄与している。

消費者物価（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合）

2027年の春闘を取り巻く環境を確認すると、有効求人倍率が 1倍を上回り、失業率が 2％台半ば
で推移するなど、労働需給は引き締まった状態が続いており、企業収益は中東情勢緊迫化の影響を
受けながらも過去最高水準の更新を続けている。消費者物価は、政府によるエネルギー負担緩和策
の影響で 2026年入り後 1％台の伸びが続いていたが、9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となり、今後上昇
ペースが加速することが確実となっている。 
賃上げの環境を過去と比較するために、労働需給（有効求人倍率）、企業収益（売上高経常利益
率）、物価（消費者物価上昇率（除く生鮮食品））について、過去平均（1985年～）からの乖離幅を
標準偏差で基準化してみると、3指標の合計は物価上昇率の鈍化を主因として前年を若干下回って
いるものの、企業収益のプラス幅が大きく拡大していることから、全体としては引き続き高水準と
なっている。賃上げの環境は引き続き良好と判断される。

### Page 6

2. 実質成長率は 2026年度 0.9％、2027年度 1.3％、2028年度 1.1％を予想

（2026年度後半は低成長が続く見通し） 
2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の主因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0.6％とマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。2027年
1-3月期は、物価上昇率が 3％台まで高まることに加え、飲食料品の消費税率引き下げ前の買い控
えの影響もあり、民間消費は前期比でマイナスとなる可能性が高く、実質 GDP成長率は前期比年
率 0.2％とほぼゼロ成長にとどまるだろう。2026年度後半の日本経済は物価高による民間消費の低
迷を主因として、低空飛行が続くことが予想される。

予測

（消費税率引き下げの影響） 
政府は 2027年 4月から 2年間に限り、飲食料品（酒類、外食を除く）の消費税率を現在の 8％か
ら 1％に引き下げるとともに、1％相当の範囲内で中低所得者向けの給付を先行導入する予定として
いる。 
食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げると、家計の負担は年間 4.3兆円程度軽減される。こ
れにより期待されるのは、物価の低下、個人消費、実質 GDPの押し上げである。ニッセイ基礎研
究所のマクロモデルによる機械的な試算によれば、食料品の消費税率を 8％から 1％に引き下げた
場合、消費者物価（総合）は▲1.4％低下し、個人消費は 0.7％、実質 GDPは 0.5％押し上げられる。 
ただし、これは消費税率の引き下げ分（7％）が全て値下げに反映された場合の試算である。こ
れまで日本では消費税率引き上げ時にほぼ 100％価格転嫁されていたが、引き下げ時にそれが当て

### Page 8

（家計の実質可処分所得は依然としてコロナ禍前を下回る） 
2026年度入り後の家計消費の低迷は、高校授業料や小学校給食費の無償化による政府消費への振
り替え、豪雨・長雨などの天候不順の影響
もあるが、より長い目でみれば、実質可処
分所得の伸び悩みが消費低迷の主因である。
家計の実質可処分所得（季節調整値）はこ
のところ持ち直しているが、依然としてコ
ロナ禍前（2019年平均）の水準を若干下回
っている。 
実質家計可処分所得の内訳をみると、雇用
者報酬は物価高による実質ベースの低下が
続いていたが、名目賃金上昇率の高まりと

### Page 9

（恒常的な所得に連動する家計消費） 
実質家計消費と実質可処分所得は、平常時には連動性が非常に高い。しかし、コロナ禍で一人当
たり 10万円の特別定額給付金が支給された際は、厳しい行動制限が課せられていたことから貯蓄
率が急上昇し、両者が大きく乖離した。また、2024年に所得税・住民税減税が行われた際も可処分
所得の増加は消費の押し上げにほとんど寄与しなかった。 
このことは、消費の持続的な回復を実現するためには、一時的な所得の増加ではなく、恒常的な
所得の増加が重要であることを示している。 
実質可処分所得は、名目賃金の高い伸びが続く一方、物価の上昇ペースが加速することから、2026
年度末にかけて低迷し、消費も停滞することが予想される。2027年度に入ると、食料品の消費税率
引き下げに伴う物価上昇率の低下によって実質
可処分所得が押し上げられ、消費も伸びを高め
るだろう。ただし、消費税率の引き下げが 2年
間の時限措置となっているため、実質所得の増
加のかなりの部分は貯蓄に回ることが想定され
る。このため、実質可処分所得の増加に比べて
家計消費の増加は小幅にとどまる公算が大きい。
家計貯蓄率は足もとの 1％程度から 2027年度に
は 2％台まで上昇するだろう。 
民間消費は 2026年度が前年比 0.0％、2027年
度が同 1.4％、2028年度が同 0.9％と予想する。

（設備投資は回復するが、供給制約が抑制要因に） 
2026年 7-9月期の設備投資は前期比 1.6％の高い伸びとなったが、4-6月期に特許権の海外譲渡に
伴う研究開発投資の減少によって落ち込んだ反動によって押し上げられた面がある。設備投資は回
復しているものの、企業収益が過去最高の更新を続けていることを考慮すれば、そのペースは緩や
かなものにとどまっている。 
日銀短観などの設備投資計画の強さに対し、GDP統計の設備投資が弱く見える理由のひとつは、
設備投資計画は名目（金額）で、GDP統計の設備投資は設備投資デフレーターで割り引いた実質で
みることが多いことである。名目設備投資は 2020年 4-6月期の 94.1兆円（季節調整済・年率換算
値）を底に着実に増加し、2026年 7-9月期には 135.4兆円となり、この間の伸びは 43.8％となって

また、企業の設備投資意欲は強いものの、人手不足などの供給制約により工事の進捗が遅れてい
ることも設備投資の抑制要因となっている可能性がある。機械受注残高と建設工事の手持ち工事高
を、それぞれ販売額、施工高の何ヵ月分に相当するかでみると、いずれも長期的に増加傾向にある。
高水準の受注残高は先行きの設備投資拡大を示唆すると同時に、人手不足などの供給制約により機
械の生産や建設工事が進まず、計画に比べて実際の設備投資の進捗が遅れている可能性も示してい
る。

設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報
関連投資、AI・半導体関連等の成長分野への投資、Eコマース拡大に伴う建設投資などの案件を軸
に、先行きも増加傾向が続くことが予想される。ただし、資材価格の高騰や人手不足による工事の
遅れから、企業収益の高い伸びや設備投資意欲の強さに比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまる可
能性が高い。 
設備投資は 2025年度の前年比 2.2％から 2026年度に同 1.0％と減速した後、2027年度が同 2.8％、
2028年度が同 2.7％と伸びを高めると予想する。

（物価の見通し） 
消費者物価（生鮮食品を除く総合、以下コア CPI）は、2026年 1月に前年比 1.9％と 2022年 3月
以来、3年 10ヵ月ぶりの 2％割れとなった後、1％
台の伸びが続いていたが、9月には同 2.1％と 9
ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となった。 
コア CPI上昇率が 1％台で推移していたのは、
主として補助金政策によって物価上昇率が押し
下げられているためである。ガソリンや電気・
ガス代等の負担緩和策、教育無償化政策などの
特殊要因は 2025年 12月以降、物価上昇率の押
し下げ要因となっており、2026年 9月の押し下

### Page 11

食料（生鮮食品を除く）は 2025年 7月の前年比 8.3％をピークに鈍化傾向が続き、2026年 8月に
同 2.7％となった後、9月には同 2.9％と 14ヵ月ぶりに上昇率が拡大した。帝国データバンクの「食
品主要 195社価格動向調査」（10/30公表）によれば、2026年（1～12月）の飲食料品の値上げ品目
数は 2万 327品目となり、前年同時期の 2万
580品目を若干下回っている。中東情勢悪化
に伴う原材料価格の高騰を受けて、先行き 3
ヵ月の値上げ品目数は 2026年 6月以降、前年
比で増加に転じているが、そのペースはやや
鈍化している。消費者物価の食料（生鮮食品
を除く）は、上昇率が高まるものの、米価格
の下落が当面続くこともあり、2025年夏場に
比べればそのペースは緩やかにとどまること
が見込まれる。 
一方、エネルギー価格は、政府による負担緩和策によって抑制されてきたが、電気・ガス料金へ
の支援策は 2026年 9月使用分（消費者物価への反映は 10月）でいったん終了した。2026年 12月
使用分からは支援策が再開されるものの、補助金額が前年を若干下回ることに加え、2026年春以降
の原油価格高騰の影響が燃料費調整単価に遅れて反映されることから、今冬の電気・ガス料金は前
年を大きく上回ることが見込まれる。さらに、政府は、ガソリン価格の高騰を抑制するため、2026
年 3月から 11月まで、補助金によってレギュラーガソリンの全国平均小売価格を 1リットル当た
り 170円程度に抑えてきたが、12月以降は補助金を段階的に縮小し、価格抑制の基準価格を毎月 5
円ずつ引き上げる方針を示した。このため、エネルギー価格は 2026年 9月に前年比▲0.6％から上
昇ペースが急加速し、2027年入り後には前年比で二桁の伸びとなり、コア CPI上昇率への寄与度は
1％を超えるだろう。 
今回の見通しでは、電気・ガス料金への支
援策については、2027年夏以降も補助金を段
階的に縮小しながら継続し、ガソリン補助金
については、価格抑制の基準価格を 2027年 2
月に 1リットル当たり 185円まで段階的に引
き上げた後、同水準で据え置くことを前提と
している。

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

Lotes released its 3Q26 results on Nov 12th and held a results 
call on Nov 13th. Key takeaways from management include: 
(1) Lotes expects GM to begin recovering starting from 4Q26 along 
with the ramp up of AMD Venice CPU, with 4Q26 GM expected to 
reach similar levels as 2Q26 (GM was 44.8% in 3Q26, down from 
45.5% in 2Q26), and expects 4Q26 revenue to see low single digit 
QoQ growth. 
(2) Management is positive on the outlook for its server business in 
2027, expecting YoY growth to accelerate compared with 2026 
(server business with 57% YoY in 2026E per GSe), and expects 
server business to account for 60%+ of total 2027 revenue (vs. 54% 
in 2026E per GSe). The solid growth will be driven by (1) server unit 
growth of 10-15% in 2027 (up from ~10% in 2026), (2) ASP increase 
across new server CPU products (including server CPU sockets, PCIe 
cables and connectors), and (3) rising contribution from new 
AI-related products (including QD, SOCAMM and new NPO 
solutions). 
(3) The company is conservative on its 2027 GM outlook, indicating 
that overall GM is unlikely to reach the 50%+ level, mainly due to a 
higher contribution from margin-dilutive products (QD and 
SOCAMM) and delay in the ramp up of next gen server CPU products 
(MP timing for Intel Oak Stream has been delayed and will begin by 
late 1Q27 or early 2Q27). 
(4) Despite QD being margin dilutive (QD with 35-40% GM per GSe), 
management is positive that this product will drive revenue/EPS 
growth going forward. Management indicated that QD contributed 
~2% of 3Q26 revenue (with 150k+ pieces of monthly shipment per 
GSe vs. ~15k pieces of shipment in 2025). Management expects 
revenue contribution from QD will increase to 3-5% by December 
and further expand to ~10% in 2027. UTR for QD is currently full, 
and the company plans to double the capacity over the coming

### Page 3

quarters.
(5) The company expects NPO products to enter MP in 2027, although their revenue
contribution is likely to be <2%. Management anticipates revenue contribution to
increase by multiple folds in 2028, driven by broader adoption of 6.4T NPO products,
which are expected to have higher ASP than the mainstream 3.2T NPO products in 2027.
(6) For the CPU platform, Lotes mentioned Eagle Stream’s penetration is currently at
~50% (was 40-50% in 2Q26), while Birch Stream’s penetration is trending upward to

~50% (was ~40% in 2Q26). For AMD, SP5 remains mainstream while current penetration
for Venice is at <3%, and is expected to reach 3-5% by 2026 end. The company now
expects AMD Venice penetration to average 20% in 2027 (previously expect to reach
30-40% by 2027 end) while Intel’s Oak Stream is expected to reach an average
penetration of 10-15% in 2H27 (previously expect to reach 20-30% by 2027 end).
Investment view
We remain relatively cautious about Lotes’ near term business outlook, and we expect

limited room for meaningful margin improvement in the near term, mainly due to (1)
CPU socket shipments remain heavily focused toward legacy products, as the ramp up of
next gen CPU platforms (including AMD Venice and Intel Oak Stream CPU) continues to
face delays, and we believe the delays are partly due to potential shortages of key
components across the general server supply chain (including ABF substrate, memory,
PCB/CCL, etc), (2) growing contribution from margin-dilutive products starting from

2H26 (including QD and SOCAMM, which accounted for 4-5% of total 3Q26 revenue per
GSe), and (3) rising material cost could weigh on profitability as the company has limited
pricing power to fully pass on cost increases without risking market share.
However, we remain constructive on the outlook for general servers (we now expect
general server volume to increase by 20%/12%/8% YoY in 2026E-28E, was 14%/6%/6%
previously, see here), which we believe Lotes is the key beneficiary with its solid market

share in the server CPU socket market. We expect Lotes’ server revenue to see 38%
2026-28E CAGR (tracking above the 28% CAGR seen during the previous high-growth
server cycle in 2019-22), and we continue to believe server is the key margin expansion
driver given its above corporate average margin (general server socket GM at ~60% vs.
3Q26 total GM of 45%) and >55% revenue contribution throughout 2026E-28E.
Overall, we believe Lotes’ current share price has largely priced in the positive outlook

for general servers (the stock is trading at 16x+ 2027E P/E based on GSe/BBG
consensus, which is +2 STDV above the average P/E during the previous high growth
server cycle in 2019-22). The revenue contribution from higher margin new products
(including next gen CPU sockets, GPU sockets, and NPO products) is likely to remain
limited in the near-term.
We maintain our Neutral rating and lower our 12m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT1,670).
3Q26 overview
Lotes’ 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 23%/15% lower than GSe and BBG consensus, with

GM 1.6ppt/1.0ppt lower than GSe/BBG consensus, which was driven by higher material
costs and higher contribution from new AI-related products with lower margins. The
OPEX to sales ratio was 2.7ppt/2.4ppt higher than GSe and BBG consensus, due to
increasing R&D spending and labor costs. For earnings, Lotes’ NI was 18%/15% lower
14 November 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Lotes (3533.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

than GSe/BBG consensus, mainly driven by the lower than expected GM.

Earnings revisions: 
We revise down our 2026E revenue by 5% to factor in 3Q26 actual results and revise 
down our 2027E revenue by 1% to factor in delay in new CPU socket products, but revise 
up our 2028E/2029E revenue estimates by 2%/2% to factor in the 
better-than-expected contribution from the server business. We revise down our 
2026/27/28/29E GM estimates by 1.2/2.2/1.2/0.9ppt to factor in the ongoing rising 
material costs and delay in new CPU socket products. Overall, we revise down our 
2026/27/28E earnings estimates by 11/8/1%. 
Valuation:  
Maintain Neutral rating and revise down our 12-m TP to NT$1,640 (from NT$1,670), 
based on an unchanged P/E of 16.0x (which is at the company’s average valuation since 
2020) and applied to 2027E EPS (rolled over from 4Q26-3Q27E), and factor in our 
earnings revisions.

### Page 5

Lotes is a connector supplier mainly focusing on the niche CPU socket market with more 
than 40% share in 2024, but we see pressure for its market share outlook in the coming 
years considering the intensifying competition in the server CPU market. We maintain 
our positive view on the company’s GM expansion potential longer term, which we 
believe will be mainly driven by better product mix (Venice server CPU to be released in 
2H26 and Oak Stream server CPU to be released in 2027, both with better ASP and 
margin). Meanwhile, Lotes’ CPU socket product line’s ASP/GM are higher than the 
company average, and we see scope for both to grow even faster in the new product 
generation given the larger socket size and pin count required. However, the company’s 
new product line, Quick Disconnects (UQD or MQD) for liquid cooling systems, is likely to 
deliver very limited revenue/gross profit contribution in the coming years (0.6/1.2% 
revenue and 0.4/0.8% GP contribution in 2026E/2027E) given thermal solution 
providers typically ship cold plates as integrated modules and leading thermal suppliers 
tend to secure QD supply in-house or through affiliated suppliers. We are Neutral rated 
on Lotes considering the share loss risk in server sockets and the shares trading at the 
historical peak P/E level, making valuation less attractive, despite the company’s solid 
revenue/GM expansion trend driven by socket upgrading demand. Key risks: (1) 
stronger-/weaker-than-expected server shipment growth which would raise/lower 
server CPU socket demand; (2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs without 
product pricing adjustment; (3) slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related 
contribution.

Valuation methodology: Our 12-month target price of NT$1,640 is based on a target 
P/E of 16.0x (in line with the company’s average valuation since 2020) applied to our 
2027E EPS.  
Key upside/downside risks: (1) Stronger-/weaker-than-expected server CPU demand, 
(2) lower-/higher-than-expected component costs, and (3) 
slower-/faster-than-expected AI server-related contribution.

Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.

Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
Component supply to improve: While component supply could stay tight near
term, Delta expects to secure bigger volume in the coming quarters, so it should
have better order fulfillment ahead. Oct-26 monthly sales seems to point to double-
digit% QoQ revenue growth in 4Q26, in our view, and it is likely to see better than
seasonal growth in 1Q27. It expects the component supply tightness to start easing

in 2Q27.
HVDC contribution minor  near term: It should start to ship HVDC power racks in
4Q26, but the contribution should be relatively small. For the GPU platform,
scheduled for 2H27, Delta sees 80VDC as the only reference design as of now,
although it does not seem to be finalized yet. Our conversations indicate most
investors do not have that as the base case, so there could be upside to consensus
estimates in 2028, if that ends up being the case.

Fuel cell another driver longer term: Delta will have some pilot production in late
2026. Its BoD has approved a greenfield project for the fuel cell business. This fits
well into its power delivery business, especially amid the power constraints that
hyperscalers are being confronted with now.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). To satisfy regulatory requirements, we correspond Overweight, our most positive stock rating, with a

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

研 究 報 告                
旺矽(6223.TT) 
產業：電子–半導體 
探針卡需求強勁挹注短期營運動能強勁、CPO 設備進度如期  
 投資評價與建議 
目前探針卡廠商評價普遍修正至20~35倍，尤其以短期營運相對穩健之廠商
(如精測、旺矽)仍相對有撐，而我們觀察公司探針卡需求強勁支撐短期營運動
能強勁，並重申CPO設備驗證進度如期、客戶數持續增加，故仍看好評價仍有
望回到合理區間之中上緣。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率
維持近3Q26之高檔水準，觀察探針卡BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，且擴產
計畫不變。基於公司10月營收達22.3億元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，
預估營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年：
(1)VPC和MEMS明年底有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，擴充幅度仍具有上
修空間，當前VPC主要客戶未有升級至MEMS規劃，至於MEMS主要貢獻
則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU；(2)insertion 2客戶將於12月通知驗證結
果，1H27有望獲得量產機訂單，至於insertion 3已如期出貨工程機給3間
客戶，並預期1H27將陸續獲得量產機訂單，惟公司預估CPO設備營收顯
著貢獻時間點將落在2028年，故我們同步調整營收、獲利。 
 投資風險 
CPO進展不如預期、探針卡擴產針數下修。 
主要財務數據及預估值

| 營運概況 
3Q26獲利優於預期：3Q26營收季增12.8%至59億元，連續七季創下歷史新高，毛利率61.7%，稅後EPS 19.18元，
其中營收、獲利均優於預期，推估主因為探針卡產品組合改善，加上於2Q26進行之探針卡報價調整完整貢獻3Q
營收(包含CPC漲價20%、取消VPC和MEMS 10%價格優惠)，另公司營業費用管控得宜也同步挹注獲利。 
估4Q26營收有望雙位數季增：公司預估4Q26營收有望季增5~10%、毛利率有望維持近3Q26之高檔水準，我們
推估動能包含：(1)維持VPC、MEMS月產能今年底分別擴至200萬針、350萬針之進展；(2)訂單出貨則觀察探針卡
BB Ratio均維持高檔水準，其中CPC今年前10個月維持滿載、目前BB Ratio接近1，至於MEMS、VPC則超過1，
考量公司探針卡交期已明顯延⻑至4~6個月，隱含本季探針卡營收仍有望持續提升。基於公司10月營收達22.3億
元創下新高、財測⼀向較為保守，預估本季營收有望季增12.7%至66.6億元，毛利率61.8%，稅後EPS 21.82元。 
積極擴增MEMS產能、重申CPO客戶需求與進度如期：展望2027年，我們觀察：(1)探針卡中，明年CPC月產能維
持50萬針、VPC和MEMS明年底則有望擴至250萬針、600萬針，產能擴充幅度具有上修空間，且強調2028年維持
積極擴產策略，訂單貢獻中，VPC主要客戶近年未有升級至MEMS探針卡之規劃，預期該客戶AI ASIC訂單將支撐
明年VPC需求，至於MEMS明年主要貢獻則來自於美系客戶伺服器CPU，且公司持續爭取潛在客戶AI ASIC訂單，
另規劃於2H27量產之PCB廠有望挹注探針卡成本優化；(2)CPO設備中，insertion 2如我們先前所提，於3Q26底
～4Q26初送樣至客戶端進行驗證，公司預估客戶將於12月通知驗證結果，根據目前客戶回饋正向，預期1H27有
望獲得量產機訂單，此外近期有另⼀客戶與公司接洽中(公司預估1H27獲得驗證結果)，至於insertion 3則已如期
出貨工程機給3間客戶，也將陸續交機給另3間客戶，預計1H27將陸續獲得insertion 3量產機訂單，惟考量接單至
出貨、裝機驗收之時間較⻑，公司認為CPO相關設備顯著營收貢獻時間點將落在2028年，我們則同步下修明年相
關設備之營收認列數量。綜上所述，公司預估明年營收有望年增至少50%、毛利率維持在60%以上；我們則預估
明年營收有望年增58.6%至344.6億元，較先前下修主要反映部分CPO設備遞延至2028年認列營收，毛利率63.2%，
稅後EPS 117.77元。

7788.TT 松川精密 
 趨勢不變 評價調升

[財務資訊更新，調升評價]   
   公司簡介 松川精密成立於 1974 年，主要從事繼電器之研發、製造及銷售，繼電器屬於自動控 制開關的一種，係以較小電流(訊號)連動大電流(電源)的元件。 
投資評等/目標價 重點摘要 
強力買進 348 
【前次投資建議】買進，348 
【大盤指數/股價】41040 / 178.5

➢ 高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元  
松川精密 3Q26 營收 27.35 億元，QoQ+21.04%，YoY+78.84%，毛利
率 27.97%，稅後淨利 2.26 億元，QoQ-14.23%，YoY+103.23%，EPS 
2.83 元，低於投研部預期，主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含
兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台興營收，單季營收佔比 3.6%，
且高毛利儲能、AI 繼電器相關產品佔比下滑 1 至 2 個百分點；2)高價原
料成本影響：上半年白銀曾衝上每盎司 121.6 美元的歷史天價，公司 8~9
月出貨產品採用高價白銀原料。 
➢ 最壞情況已過，4Q26 獲利重返單季新高 
10 月營收 8.49 億元，MoM-3.19%，YoY+53.62%，達成率 32%，符
合預期，部分產品出貨受船期影響，致營收遞延認列，整體單季營收與
原先預估差異不大。毛利率方面，下半年白銀每季平均跌幅 12%~14%，
目前回落至 50 美元以下，原料平均成本已下降，10 月高價原料衝擊影
響性降低，毛利率回升，預期單季最壞情況已過，4Q26 毛利率回升至
29.11%，EPS 3.33 元，獲利重返成長。 
➢ AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，必須額外配置
高壓直流接觸器，技術門檻遠高於傳統產品，單價達到交流繼電器的 20
倍至 30 倍，投研部推測每機櫃至少需配置 12 至 28 顆不等，單機櫃產
值成長 198%。松川 225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，直流接
觸器將是松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼
電器/接觸器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
➢ 評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
投研部預估松川精密 2026 年/2027 年 EPS 分別為 11.68 元及 15.58
元，2027 年各應用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利
率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優
於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方面，目前松川本益比僅 11
倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投研部將松川投
資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

高價庫存原料侵蝕毛利，3Q26 EPS 2.83 元，低於預期

主要歸因於毛利率季減 2.98 個百分點，包含兩大因素：1)產品組合變化：完整認列台

個股報告 
民主黨勝選有利綠能產業發展，儲能剛性需求強 
根據產業統計，2026 年前三季全球儲能總併網量估計達 220 至 240 GWh，系統出貨
量約 420 GWh。受惠於第四季併網旺季，BloombergNEF 於 10 月將 2026 全年新增
裝機預估上調至 188 GW / 535 GWh，並預期 2027 年將進一步成長至 209 GW / 638 
GWh，容量年增 19.3%。太陽能方面，全球新增裝機在 2025 年創高後，2026 年受
中國電網消納限制影響，預計微幅回落至 584 至 638 GW；但海外市場逆勢成長 15%
至 313 GW。展望 2027 年，全球裝機預估將回升至 618 GW，產業呈現「光弱儲強」
趨勢，加速光儲一體化建置。 
綜合上述，全球光儲兩大核心市場皆集中於中美兩國，加上美國民主黨於 2026 年期
中選舉勝出並奪下國會主導權，綠能與清潔能源政策延續性獲得有力支撐，可望進一
步推升當地光儲建置動能。中美兩地領導廠商均為松川精密的核心客戶，受惠於美系
客戶新一代儲能櫃容量由 3.9 MWh 提升至 5 MWh，工作電流增加帶動接觸器規格與
單價雙雙看漲，預估 2027 年松川在儲能、太陽能及微電網業務將維持 10%~15%的
成長。 
AI 伺服器高壓直流架構，繼電器/接觸器產值增加近兩倍 
AI 伺服器從 54V 升級至±400V 或 800V 高壓直流架構，機櫃配電單元(PDU)面臨嚴
峻的高壓電弧安全挑戰，必須額外配置高壓直流接觸器(DC Contactor)，為在毫秒內
強制切斷(滅弧)800V 高壓直流電弧，產品內部高度仰賴磁吹滅弧設計或真空密封充氣
(氫氣／氮氣)工藝，技術門檻遠高於傳統交流繼電器(AC Relay)，單價達到交流繼電器
的 20 倍至 30 倍。在用量方面，每座機櫃 PDU 至少需配置 12 顆，主流高階規格更
需 24 顆至 28 顆，單機櫃直流接觸器產值為既有交流繼電器的 2 至 3 倍。松川
225A/900V 產品已率先邁入小量出貨階段，未來隨著高壓直流架構全面放量，直流接
觸器將正式擔綱松川 2027 年的核心成長引擎。在額外增加直流接觸器需求下，投研
部推測高壓直流架構(不含自動轉換開關 ATS)，AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸器單機櫃產值將
從 7,000 元增加至 20,900 元，YoY+198%，預估松川 2027 年 AI 伺服器繼電器/接觸
器營收成長 50%~60%，營收佔比 20.2%。 
預估松川 2027 年 EPS 15.58 元，評等調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元 
預估松川精密 2026 年營收 95.52 億元，YoY+48.49%，毛利率 28.80%，稅後淨利
9.31 億元，YoY+246.03%，EPS 11.68 元；預估 2027 年 113.14 億元，YoY+18.45%，
毛利率 30.53%，稅後淨利 12.43 億元，YoY+33.42%，EPS 15.58 元。2027 年各應
用面展望樂觀，儲能及 AI 伺服器仍是主軸，雖然毛利率較原先下修 1 個百點，但上修
直流接觸器產值預估，加上費用控管優於預期，2027 年整體獲利小幅上修。評價方
面，目前松川本益比僅 11 倍，處於歷史 PE 區間 10X~30X 下緣，投資價值浮現，投
研部將松川投資評等，由買進調升至強力買進，目標價 348 元(2027F PE23X)。

 절대 (달러환산) (23.8) 1.5  (18.0)

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

### Page 1

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

EARNINGS REVIEW Company Analysis | 건설/원전 | 2026. 10. 30  
숫자로 증명하는 중

3Q26 Review: 에너빌부문 믹스개선, 두산밥캣 호실적 
두산에너빌리티 3분기 매출액은 4조 4,494억원으로 전년동기대비 14.7% 증가, 컨센
서스에 부합했다. 영업이익은 2,612억원으로 전년동기대비 90.6% 증가, 컨센서스 영
업이익 2,464억원 대비 6.0% 상회하는 호실적을 기록했다. 에너빌리티부문의 원자
력, 가스터빈 매출 증가에 따른 믹스 개선 효과와 두산밥캣의 가격정책 효과에 힘입은 
마진 개선으로 안정적인 실적 기조가 이어지고 있어 긍정적이다. 분기 환평가손실로 
인해 순이익은 여타 수출기업과 유사하게 컨센서스 대비 하회했다. 에너빌부문의 수주
는 누계 10조원으로 목표대비 75%를 채웠으며, 수주 대기중인 안건들을 고려할 때 
당분간 수주 업사이클이 몇년간 지속될 것임이 분명한 시점이다. 
기회가 아닌 경쟁력, 모멘텀이 아닌 수주  
두산에너빌리티에 대해 투자의견 Buy 유지하나, 목표주가를 95,000원으로 하향했다. 
목표 EBITDA는 2035년 EBITDA에 WACC 8%를 적용, 목표 EV/EBITDA는 Global 
Peers인 GE Vernova의 2026년 EV/EBITDA 멀티플에서 자회사 실적 변동성을 고
려해 10% 할인 적용했다. 호실적과 수주 증가에도 GEV의 멀티플 감소와 순차입금 
증가로 인한 NAV 감소를 밸류에이션에 반영한 결과이다. 전일 주가는 한수원의 체코 
원전 시공사 협상 결렬로 인해 7.65% 하락 마감했다. 시공 수주는 아쉽지만 두산에너
빌리티의 체코 원전 기자재 납품에는 전혀 영향이 없으며, 글로벌 전력 수요 증가로 경
제적해자인 대형 가스 터빈 수급 쇼티지는 당분간 지속될 전망임을 고려할 때 전일 주
가는 낙폭 과대로 판단한다. 1) 두산에너빌리티의 가스터빈은 이미 2031년 출하 물량
에 대한 예약 주문이 마무리되고 있으며, 2) 또한 AP1000의 우량한 수주 파이프라인
과, 3) 뉴스케일파워, 테라파워, 롤스로이스, X Energy 등의 SMR 제작을 위한 협업 역
시 지속되고 있다는 점에 주목한다. 전일 기사를 통해 뉴스케일파워 SMR 1호 제작 계
약을 체결했다는 소식을 전했으며, 이제 에너지 트렌지션은 기회가 아닌 회사의 경쟁
력으로, 가스터빈과 원전은 모멘텀이 아니라 수주로 입증되고 있다.  
 Analyst 김세련 
sally.kim@ls-sec.co.kr

Document 277: Anthropic“超级IPO”：过半机构投资者“一股未得”，散户“凶猛买卖”

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic“超级IPO”：过半机构投资者“一股未得”，散户“凶猛买卖”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

超 1200 家机构抢购 Anthropic IPO ，逾半数颗粒⽆收，欧洲投资者⼏乎全军覆没。散⼾则
以 7-8 亿美元总交易额创 2012 年以来 IPO 散⼾交易量历史纪录，但双向买卖⽽⾮单边做多。
摘要：
Anthropic 史上最⼤规模 IPO 在机构与散⼾之间制造了截然不同的两个世界：超过半数机构投资
者颗粒⽆收，⽽散⼾则以创纪录的交易量同时扮演买家与卖家。
据彭博最新报道，约 1200 家机构投资者参与了此次认购，但超过半数未能获得任何配股。
Anthropic 此次 IPO 连同七位联合创始⼈共募资 1073 亿美元，其中包括来⾃英伟达、⾕歌及超微
半导体（ AMD ）等投资者的 260 亿美元私募配售，从 SpaceX ⼿中夺得史上最⼤规模⾸次公开募
股的桂冠。按发⾏价计算， Anthropic 完全摊薄市值达 1.5 万亿美元。
与此同时，散⼾投资者的总交易额（含买⼊与卖出）预计将达到 7 亿⾄ 8 亿美元，超越 SpaceX 上
市⾸⽇的 6.69 亿美元，创下 Vanda Research 追溯⾄ 2012 年以来所有 IPO 中散⼾交易额的历史最

⾼纪录。
华尔街⻅闻⽂章写道，上市⾸⽇， Anthropic 股价⼀度较发⾏价飙升差 34% ，盘中最⾼触及
115.10 美元，涨幅扩⼤到约 38.7% ，此后持续回吐涨幅，刷新⽇低时回到⾄ 92.80 美元下⽅、涨
幅收窄⾄不⾜ 12% ，到收盘，较发⾏价涨约 17.1% ，相当于回吐开盘时的半数涨幅。
机构配股严重向美国本⼟倾斜，欧洲投资者⼏乎 “ 颗粒⽆收 ”
据报道，知情⼈⼠透露，此次配股在地域分布上严重偏向美国本⼟投资者，⼤量来⾃美国以外
的订单未获任何配售，其中欧洲投资者受影响尤为突出。
La Financiere de l'Echiquier 科技基⾦经理 Christophe Pouchoy 表⽰：
“ 对于⾝处欧洲的投资者⽽⾔，参与美国 IPO 并获得配股本就相当困难，在这种极度抢⼿的交易
中尤为如此。承销商⾯对巨⼤需求不得不做出取舍，绝⼤多数欧洲投资者最终未获任何配售。 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
95 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
相⽐之下，据彭博此前报道， SpaceX IPO 时约有三分之⼀的下单投资者未获配股， Anthropic 此
次未获配股的机构投资者⽐例明显更⾼。
此次 IPO 是⾸家以开发最先进 AI 模型为核⼼业务的公司登陆公开市场， Anthropic 完全摊薄市值
达 1.5 万亿美元，低于 SpaceX 上市时 1.8 万亿美元的估值。
散⼾创纪录双向交易，净买⼊规模仍逊于 SpaceX
散⼾投资者在 Anthropic 上市⾸⽇展现出异乎寻常的交投热情。据 Vanda Research 的 Viraj Patel
介绍，截⾄当⽇下午晚些时候，⾮专业投资者对 Anthropic 股票的净买⼊规模预计将在收盘前达
到约 5000 万⾄ 6000 万美元，有望跻⾝史上最⼤单⽇净买⼊记录之列 —— 但仍低于 SpaceX 上市

⾸⽇的 1.18 亿美元以及 Coinbase Global Inc. 2021 年上市时的 9200 万美元。
然⽽，若以买卖双向合计的总交易额衡量，散⼾的参与程度则已全⾯超越此前所有记录。 Patel
表⽰，散⼾总交易额预计达到 7 亿⾄ 8 亿美元，超过 SpaceX 的 6.69 亿美元，是其数据库中追溯⾄
2012 年以来所有 IPO 中散⼾交易额的最⾼纪录。
Patel 在早些时候的报告中指出，“ 散⼾正在⼤⼒交易这只股票 ” ，并补充称投资者 “ 双向参与，⽽
⾮单边买⼊ ” 。
在散⼾经纪商层⾯，美国最⼤零售经纪商 Robinhood Markets Inc. ⾸席经纪官 Steven Quirk 在彭
博 ETFs in Depth ⼤会间隙表⽰，散⼾投资者对 Anthropic 的配股需求 “ 远超 ” 实际所获份额。他表
⽰， Robinhood 获得了 “ 不错的配额 ” ，但上市⾸⽇的交易仍以机构投资者为主导。
尽管如此， Quirk 表⽰，每⼀位希望持有 Anthropic 股票的 Robinhood 客⼾都⾄少获得了⼀股。

另外，值得关注的是，据 Patel 介绍，散⼾投资者为筹集资⾦购⼊ Anthropic 股票，选择出售其他
个股，抛售范围覆盖整个 “AI 板块” ，其中 AMD 跌幅居前 —— 此前Hunterbrook Capital 宣布做空
该股，理由是其在中国存在潜在⻛险业务。与此同时，⻓期受散⼾追捧的英伟达买盘也 “ 急剧萎
缩 ” 。

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic开盘大涨，一切看似完美！随后风云突变，美股跳水，发生了什么？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-20

### Page 1

周四， Anthropic 史上最⼤规模 IPO 登陆市场，开盘⼀度⼤涨逾 38% ， AI 交易看似迎来强⼒
背书。然⽽，这场盛宴来得快，去得也快 —— 美股全天⾼开低⾛，标普 500 收跌 2% ，纳指
跌 2.25% 。做空机构突袭 AMD 、逾千亿 IPO 抽⼲流动性、负 Gamma 仓位创五年极值 ……AI
盛宴变成了美股压⼒测试。
摘要：
11 ⽉ 19 ⽇， Anthropic 以 83 美元定价上市，开盘即飙升⾄ 111.50 美元，全场沸腾。但 Anthropic 开
盘即是⾼点，股价随即⼀路回落，收盘 97 美元，涨幅收窄⾄ 17% 。
与此同时，美股⼤盘也是冲⾼回落的⾛势 —— 纳斯达克从开盘涨逾 2% ，到收盘跌 2.25% ，单⽇
振幅超过 4 个百分点。标普 500 当⽇蒸发市值约 1.3 万亿美元，与 Anthropic 此次 IPO 募资规模⾼度
吻合。
美股盘中，做空机构 Hunterbrook 发布报告做空 AMD ，拖累 AI 板块整体承压；逾千亿美元的 IPO

融资规模抽⾛市场流动性，叠加做市商处于极端负 gamma 状态及⽉度期权到期压⼒，成为市场
剧烈波动的核⼼推⼿。 Anthropic 上市⾸⽇的剧情，本该是 AI 信仰的加固时刻，但却意外演变成
⼀场压⼒测试。
开盘：⼀切看起来很美好
11 ⽉ 19 ⽇周四美股开盘前，市场弥漫着久违的乐观⽓氛。
Anthropic 以 83 美元的发⾏价挂牌，⾼于此前路演区间上限，对应估值约 1.1 万亿美元，创下 IPO
史上最⾼募资规模，消息提振隔夜美股期货全线⾛⾼。
美东时间早盘 9 时 30 分，纳斯达克综合指数随即冲⾼逾 2% ，标普 500 同步上涨，市场情绪⼀度⾼
涨。彼时，市场普遍将这笔史上最⼤规模的科技 IPO 解读为 AI 交易依然健康的有⼒信号。
Direxion 资本市场主管 Jake Behan 表⽰： “ 此前市场⼀直在讨论 AI 交易是否已经⾛过了头，所以
先看到英伟达超预期，再看到交易员愿意追逐 Anthropic ⾼于发⾏价的价格，这是相当值得关注
的信号。”“ 这并不能终结所有估值争议，但它有⼒地表明，市场还没准备好放弃这笔交易。 ”

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

然⽽，好景不⻓。
开盘即⾼点
就在开盘后不久，⼀条彭博等媒体报道的消息迅速在华尔街交易台蔓延：“Anthropic IPO 据报
⼏乎未向⼤多数机构买家配售。 ”
这条消息让市场瞬间警觉。 ZeroHedge 分析指出，这背后有两种解读：
要么是承销商对需求极度⾃信，刻意压缩机构配额以确保上市后股价爆发；
要么，是机构根本不愿以 83 美元的价格接盘，最终只能将筹码塞给对价格更不敏感的散⼾。
市场似乎倾向于后者。
上午⾄午间： AMD 暴跌，芯⽚板块遭殃
就在⼤盘摇摇欲坠之际，另⼀颗 “ 炸弹 ” 引爆。
做空机构 Hunterbrook 发布报告，披露其已做空 AMD，并指控该公司持续向被美国列⼊⿊名单
的企业提供芯⽚固件更新服务，同时与此前引发英伟达监管争议的部分企业存在关联。
AMD 随即回应： “ 这些说法歪曲了 AMD 的业务实践和合作关系。 ”
但声明并未能⽌住跌势。 AMD 单⽇跌幅超 9% ，拖累整个芯⽚及 AI 板块集体⾛低。
正午时分， Anthropic 开盘即遭抛售

美东时间正午 12 时， Anthropic 正式开盘，开盘价 111.50 美元，较 83 美元发⾏价⼤涨逾 34% 。
然⽽，就在开盘的瞬间，股价便遭到抛售 ——“ 利好兑现 ” 的逻辑应声⽽⾄。与此同时，⼤盘也⼏
乎在同⼀时刻跌⾄⽇内低点。
据《纽约时报》报道，这⼀⾛势与 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉上市时如出⼀辙，彼时同样出现了开盘冲⾼
后快速回落的⾏情，投资者对此早有⼼理准备。
Anthropic 最终以每股 97 美元收盘，涨幅收窄⾄ 17% 。

### Page 3

下午：跌势加速，全线溃退
美东午后，跌势进⼀步蔓延。
纳指从开盘涨逾 2% ，⼀路反转⾄收跌 2.25% ，单⽇振幅超 4 个百分点。标普 500 收跌 2% ， 11 个
板块全线飘绿，市值单⽇蒸发约 1.3 万亿美元 —— 与 Anthropic 上市时约 1.3 万亿美元的估值规模
⼏乎相当。
这场抛售，不只是科技股的问题。标普 500 消费可选板块所有成分股在午后均告下跌，领跌的是
Williams-Sonoma—— 该公司公布的第三季度业绩显⽰，销售额未达分析师预期。强劲的消费⽀
出⼀直是今年美国经济韧性的重要⽀柱，投资者对消费放缓的信号格外敏感。
当⽇美股 VIX 恐慌指数升⾄近 28 ， VVIX （波动率的波动率）突破 125 ，标普 500 ⼀个⽉隐含相关
性同步攀升 —— 分析称，这些指标均指向⼀个⼴泛的去⻛险环境，⽽⾮个股层⾯的孤⽴性抛
售。
这也是标普 500 连续第六个交易⽇下跌，为 2024 年 4 ⽉以来最⻓连跌纪录。

### Page 4

幕后推⼿还有负 Gamma 与流动性抽离
这场暴跌，还有多条隐藏的线索。
第⼀，技术结构极度脆弱。
野村证券策略师 Charlie McElligott 此前已预警，在英伟达财报、 Anthropic 上市与期权到期 “ 四重
⻛险 ” 叠加之下，做市商的 “ 负 Gamma” 敞⼝已升⾄ 2019 年以来最⾼⽔平。
当做市商处于负 Gamma 状态时，他们被迫顺势对冲 —— 市场下跌时卖出，进⼀步放⼤跌幅。
0DTE （零⽇到期）期权在下午的流量⼤量涌⼊，进⼀步压垮了较⻓期限的⽀撑。
第⼆， IPO 本⾝ “ 抽⼲ ” 了市场流动性。
这笔 IPO 合计募资 813 亿美元（含 267 亿美元的 vendor placement ，总计逾 1000 亿美元）。交易台
消息称，市场参与者为筹集认购资⾦，抛售了⻩⾦、铜、加密货币以及英伟达等 AI 代理资产。这
⼀说法与盘⾯吻合：⻩⾦跌破 3800 美元、铜价⼤跌约 5% 、加密货币全线回调。
第三，消费板块拖累更为明显。
标普 500 所有⾮必需消费品股票在午后均告下跌。强劲的消费⽀出⼀直是今年美国经济韧性的核

⼼⽀柱，这⼀信号令投资者对消费放缓的担忧升温。
第四，年末获利了结压⼒。
与此同时，机构投资者也有⾃⼰的盘算。《纽约时报》指出，尽管近期市场波动加剧，投资者今
年整体仍享有丰厚回报。感恩节前后，交易员倾向于锁定利润，因为年末市场流动性趋于收
缩，届时以理想价格成交的难度会更⼤。
乐观者还未放弃
⾼盛分析师 Rich Privorotsky 在当天早间发出预警， “ 今天感觉很重要 ” 。他指出，机构对科技股
的净仓位约为 18% ，远低于指数 29% 的权重，处于五年极值附近的低配状态 —— 这意味着只要
市场不⼤幅下跌，就可能触发补仓式轧空⾏情。
Direxion 也表⽰，市场对 Anthropic IPO 整体上仍持积极态度，英伟达超预期财报与 Anthropic 的
顺利上市，共同提供了 “AI 交易尚未终结 ” 的信⼼背书。

### Page 5

市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI.
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy.
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.
